Fletcher Building Limited

FBU

Fletcher Building supplies building materials and distribution in New Zealand and Australia. Q1 core volumes improved, but housing contribution remains weak and a broad recovery is not expected before calendar 2027. The source-backed analyst base scenarios, not issuer guidance, yield FYJun2027/FYJun2028 EPS of NZ$0.19218/NZ$0.21175 using the historical 1,124m diluted-share denominator. At the delayed NZ$3.35 exchange quote, conditional own P/E is 17.43x/15.82x. Holding the own FY1 multiple unchanged gives an FY2 reference of 1.101818x; this is not matched-peer fair value or an expected return. Current gross dilution, recurring significant costs, SBC and cash conversion remain unresolved. The recovery thesis fails if stronger volumes do not produce recurring common earnings and cash.

More details

Catalyst

# Fletcher Building Limited (NZX/ASX: FBU) ; conditional WATCH model **As of 14 October 2026.** FBU ordinary shares are cross-listed; count the issuer's shares once. This is an analyst scenario model, not issuer guidance, not peer-parity valuation, and not an actionable trade recommendation. ## Earnings bridge and scenarios FY26 continuing operations produced NZ$414m EBIT before significant items, composed of Light Building Products NZ$246m, Heavy Building Materials NZ$108m, Distribution NZ$12m, Residential & Development NZ$94m, and corporate costs NZ$46m. Continuing EBITDA before significant items was NZ$730m. However, the Q1 FY27 report says volume was ahead year-on-year in most core businesses, while pipelines softened after customer pre-buying; management expects an uncertain first half, minimal Residential & Development contribution to half-year earnings, and no meaningful underlying volume recovery until calendar 2027. The model starts from those segment EBITs and uses the following explicitly assumed segment trajectories, finance costs and 21.45% recurring tax rate (derived from FY26 adjusted continuing earnings). EPS uses 1,124m diluted weighted-average shares, retaining SBC in EBIT and assuming no new share issuance. Low/base/high FY28 reference multiples of 8x/12x/16x are analyst sensitivities only; no matched peer median was verified. | Scenario | FY27 EBIT | FY28 EBIT | FY27 diluted EPS | FY28 diluted EPS | FY28 reference P/E | FY28 value | MOIC vs NZ$3.35 close | |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:| | Low | NZ$363m | NZ$369m | NZ$0.152 | NZ$0.153 | 8x | NZ$1.22 | 0.37x (-63%) | | Base | NZ$400m | NZ$428m | NZ$0.192 | NZ$0.212 | 12x | NZ$2.54 | 0.76x (-24%) | | High | NZ$429m | NZ$465m | NZ$0.216 | NZ$0.245 | 16x | NZ$3.91 | 1.17x (+17%) | The FY28 price cases are undiscounted and exclude costs; the NZ$3.35 reference is a delayed historical NZX close, not executable depth. The wide multiple and earnings sensitivities are intentionally not presented as qualified fair value. FY26 reported EPS was NZ$0.212 including discontinued operations; continuing normalized basic EPS before significant items was NZ$0.211 and FY26 diluted shares were 1,124m. FY26 net operating cash flow was NZ$715m, net debt was NZ$637m at June 30, and no dividend was declared. ## Thesis and risk **Opportunity / torque:** broad volume recovery from calendar 2027 could lift core manufacturing utilization and margins and convert recent cash generation/deleveraging into earnings growth. Q1 volume improvements across most core units support the possibility, but do not establish a recovery path. **Strongest contrary case:** FY26 continuing EBIT before significant items rose 26% to NZ$414m, operating cash flow improved, net debt fell from NZ$999m to NZ$637m, and Q1 volumes were higher in most core units. A simplified portfolio could recover faster than the market expects; through-cycle earnings could exceed the base case. **Downside / kill condition:** weak first-half demand or another core-volume rollover could reduce EBIT and cash conversion while debt remains material. Reassess the recovery thesis if core volumes decline for two consecutive reporting periods, continuing EBIT/cash conversion deteriorates, or net debt rises without a funded recovery plan. **Blocker / next check:** these scenario earnings and reference multiples are analyst assumptions, not published FY27/FY28 estimates. Current diluted capitalization and aggregate SBC expense are incomplete; no timestamped executable NZX/ASX quote or account-level access evidence is verified. At FY27 half-year results and the next volume update, refresh segment earnings, financing/tax, diluted shares and debt, then obtain a sized NZX book before any actionability decision.

Risk / thesis breaker

FY26 net operating cash flow of NZD 715m, net debt reduction to NZD 637m, and Q1 volume improvement in most core businesses may show that the simplified portfolio can recover earnings before broad end-market demand normalizes; the current trailing multiple could then understate through-cycle earnings. Thesis fails if: Downgrade/kill a recovery thesis if core volumes roll over for two successive reporting periods, continuing EBIT/cash conversion deteriorates materially, or net debt rises without a funded, time-bound recovery plan.

Upside torque

Conditional reference 1.101818x; not qualified peer MOIC or expected return

CyberCatch Holdings, Inc.

CYBE

CyberCatch is subject to a board-backed all-cash acquisition by a Datavault subsidiary. The gross US$3.22 per common share is subject to corporate-action and transaction-expense adjustments; the confidential expense amount is not public, so net proceeds are unknown. At October 13 reference FX and the C$2.30 Canadian last trade, the headline ratio is about 1.991x, not an expected return or executable spread. The October 14 vote, final court approval and other conditions remain outstanding at this edition cutoff. The agreement requires aggregate consideration escrow before effectiveness and has no financing condition, but neither provision proves cash is funded today. A separate US$500,000 bridge is not acquisition funding. Warrants receive no deal consideration; option cash claims require a full schedule. The outside date is February 17, 2027. A failed vote, required escrow failure, court denial or termination breaks the thesis; standalone failure-case value is unverified.

Catalyst

# CyberCatch Holdings, Inc. (TSXV: CYBE) ; conditional transaction, non-actionable **As of 14 October 2026, 06:03 America/Vancouver, before the scheduled vote.** The August 17 arrangement agreement provides a gross US$3.22 for each common share at the Effective Time. The price is subject to corporate-action adjustments and transaction-cost adjustments. The public agreement says the company’s transaction-expense cap and accrued/estimated transaction expenses are in its confidential disclosure letter; that dollar amount is unavailable here. Thus US$3.22 is not yet verified net common-share proceeds. The agreement requires the Purchaser to place sufficient aggregate consideration with the Depositary in escrow before the Effective Time. CyberCatch’s closing conditions require compliance and Depositary confirmation of receipt. This is a future funding requirement, not evidence the money has already been deposited. The agreement has no stated financing condition, but the separate US$500,000 secured bridge loan for CyberCatch’s interim expenses is not acquisition funding. The Parent’s August 18 filing says certain support holders representing about 20% of common shares agreed to vote for the deal; that does not satisfy the full vote thresholds. The scheduled October 14 vote had not occurred at this cutoff. Required approvals include two-thirds of common-shareholder votes, two-thirds of all securityholders voting together, and a simple majority of eligible minority votes, followed by final court and other conditions. Additional conditions include no more than 5% valid dissent rights, required consents, regulatory/exchange clearances, no material adverse effect and agreed employee arrangements. Contract outside date: February 17, 2027. At Bank of Canada Oct13 USD/CAD 1.4222, the headline gross price translates to C$4.5795. Dividing by the C$2.30 Oct13 TSXV last trade gives a mechanical 1.991x ratio before transaction-expense adjustments, closing risk, timing, fees and settlement FX. It is not an expected MOIC, executable spread or downside floor. A failed deal has no verified standalone value in this model. **Security claims:** each outstanding vested/unvested option receives cash equal to max(US$3.22 minus its exercise price, zero), subject to withholding and transaction-cost adjustments; the public copy does not provide a full option count/strike schedule. Outstanding warrants are canceled for no consideration. No option/warrant payout is assumed in common-shareholder value. **Low case:** the arrangement fails, or a material transaction-expense adjustment lowers proceeds; failure-case standalone common value is unknown. **Base case:** approvals and funding/escrow are confirmed and the transaction closes at the gross stated terms before the undisclosed cost adjustment. **High case:** successful close with no material transaction-expense reduction; no higher bid is evidenced. No completion probability is assigned. **Strongest contrary case:** board/special committee recommend the all-cash proposal, supported by a fairness opinion; public filing reports a 180% premium to the unaffected August 14 price. **Kill condition:** failed required vote, court denial, material adverse effect, missed outside date, termination, or failure to make the required escrow deposit invalidates the deal thesis. The US$4.01625m termination fee is payable by CyberCatch only in specified termination events and is not a shareholder downside floor. FY1/FY2 normalized EPS and peer P/E are not applicable to this near-term transaction case; no standalone forecast, complete dilution schedule or present cash/debt bridge is verified. Next: after the vote, obtain its official result, the transaction-expense amount/cap and option schedule, final order and actual escrow confirmation before revising the conditional payout.

Risk / thesis breaker

Required approvals or buyer cash deposit fail; undisclosed transaction expenses reduce net proceeds, while unverified standalone value leaves deal-break downside unbounded in this model. Thesis fails if: Required vote or court approval fails, the cash deposit is not made, or the agreement terminates without a replacement outcome.

Upside torque

Conditional reference 1.991080x; not qualified peer MOIC or expected return

**As of 14 October 2026.** FY26 ends December 31; FY27 ends December 31, 2027. This is a source-anchored operating sensitivity, not a completed normalized EPS valuation. FY26 H1 production averaged 13,726 bopd and adjusted EBITDA was US$78.7m, or US$33.05 per sold barrel; free funds flow was US$58.1m after US$15.6m capex. The new Q3 update reports production of 11,797 bopd, YTD production of 13,076 bopd and unrestricted cash of US$115.6m. Q3 output fell 6.05% from Q2; the release is operational, not a statutory earnings/cash-flow report. Management says 25H drilling remains on schedule and targets Bretana production near 20,000 bopd by end-Q1 2027. A simple adjusted-EBITDA sensitivity applies the observed H1 sales/production ratio (95.8%) and observed Q1/H1/Q2 adjusted EBITDA per barrel (US$27.22/US$33.05/US$39.98). On explicit Q4 average production assumptions of 10k/12k/14k bopd, FY26 adjusted EBITDA is approximately US$137m/148m/162m, including actual H1 EBITDA and an estimated Q3 at the H1 rate. FY27 scenarios assume 14k/18k/20k average production and the respective historical per-barrel values, yielding approximately US$133m/208m/280m adjusted EBITDA. The FY27 high case is more optimistic than the issuer's stated end-Q1 production objective. These are analyst operating sensitivities, not guidance. EPS, owner FCF, target price and MOIC remain unassigned. Q3 financial results, D&A, tax/royalty, interest, hedge settlement, full capex and current fully diluted shares are missing; multiplying EBITDA by a peer or applying an unverified P/E would manufacture value. The C$0.58 Oct13 TSX last trade is not executable. **Opportunity / torque:** H1 production was ahead of budget, Q3 cash rose to US$115.6m, and 25H could arrest declines and restore production toward 20,000 bopd. **Strongest contrary case:** this may be a low-cost producer with demonstrated positive H1 free funds flow and enough liquidity to fund development; if 25H succeeds, production and operating cash could rise materially. **Risk / kill condition:** Q3 production declined, reported cash growth includes a like-for-like receivables/payables timing proxy of US$10.8m, and the 20,000 bopd objective is future, not achieved. Reassess if cash conversion remains weak after timing/capex/hedges, or 25H fails to restore production toward the stated objective without a funded replacement plan. **Next check:** November 9 Q3 financial statements and MD&A; reconcile cash, working capital, taxes, capex, hedges, debt and current diluted capital, and verify 25H output before assigning EPS or a target.

Q3 YTD (Dec-Aug): orders ¥37.27bn (+50.4% YoY), backlog ¥23.359bn (+76.6%); sales ¥25.271bn (+3.2%), operating profit ¥77m (vs ¥331m loss), ordinary profit ¥8m (vs ¥539m loss), net loss ¥129m (vs ¥554m loss). Report cites textile machinery orders incl. glass-cloth/AI-semiconductor prep machinery and machine-tool demand in aerospace/data centers. FY guidance was not revised (issuer says no revision). Separate Oct. 13 notice converts RMB35m loan to subsidiary equity; planned late Nov.; issuer says no current consolidated or standalone earnings impact expected. Detected official TSE limit event or oldest pending valuation refresh; not a Buy signal Issuer disclosure dated 2026-10-09 (issuer news index also lists the deal on Oct. 9, four calendar days before the Oct. 13 limit event; precise publication time was not captured). It provides for an Epson capital/business alliance and third-party allotment of 996,677 new shares at ¥301 each, gross ¥299,999,777, net proceeds about ¥296,149,777 after estimated ¥3.85m costs; payment and alliance commencement expected Oct. 27, conditional on securities registration effectiveness. Existing shares were 5,692,800 as of Mar. 31: new shares equal 17.51% of pre-issue shares; Epson's post-issue ownership/voting ratio is 14.91% (existing holders' aggregate percentage dilution 14.91%, assuming no other share changes). ¥301 matched Oct. 8 close (the last close before board resolution). Uses: ¥178m factory/capacity expansion and equipment, ¥59m building/environment for Epson-loaned inspection equipment, ¥59m joint development through Aug. 2031. Epson is an existing major customer; disclosure says trade scale has grown year by year, but specifies no guaranteed minimum purchase or contract value. Collaboration includes long-term stable supply of Epson components and joint development for inkjet printhead parts/high-function parts and next-generation materials.

Report cites textile machinery orders incl. glass-cloth/AI-semiconductor prep machinery and machine-tool demand in aerospace/data centers. FY guidance was not revised (issuer says no revision). Catalyst

# StrikePoint Gold Inc. (TSXV: SKP) ; WATCH, post-close asset case **As of 14 October 2026.** StrikePoint Gold is the TSXV issuer, not another company sharing the SKP ticker. It consolidated ten old shares into one new common share effective October 2. Its October 6 issuer release confirms the Northumberland acquisition and C$190m receipt escrow both closed: US$70m was paid to Newmont, 95m C$2 subscription receipts converted into common shares, and a 0.5% NSR was sold to Tembo for US$10m. The close materially updates the prior Oct2 delivered email, which named “SKP, StrikePoint Gold” only in its complete-security index. The Oct6 close and completed royalty sale are new fundamental events; no prior developed thesis is inferred from that index mention. After conversion, basic shares were 102.239m; 7.058m new broker warrants at C$2 bring known minimum gross claims to 109.297m before legacy options/awards. The new warrants could provide C$14.116m if all are exercised, but that cash is neither current nor guaranteed. Tembo owns 20.3m shares (about 19.9%) and receives board/technical committee rights. The company sold a 0.5% NSR and may buy back half of it for US$25m at a stated future date. A transaction-flow bridge; not a cash balance; starts with C$190m gross proceeds, subtracts Canaccord’s 3%-6% commission (C$5.7m-C$11.4m) and Arlington’s C$3.855m fee, converts and subtracts the US$70m purchase and adds the US$10m royalty proceeds at October 6 reference FX. The resulting C$89.4m-C$95.1m is before subscription-agent expenses, other fees, exploration/development spending and unreported balances. Current unrestricted cash and debt are not verified. The project has a reported 2.86Moz indicated and 1.57Moz inferred AuEq mineral resource, but no reserve or feasibility-backed economics. Two additional US$25m payments depend on feasibility and commercial-production milestones. Low/base/high scenarios therefore remain event/asset cases only; EPS, owner FCF, target price and MOIC are null. **Strongest contrary case:** the financing escrow did release and the asset purchase and NSR sale completed, providing a funded runway and a large Nevada resource on a brownfield private-land site. **Main risk / kill condition:** resource does not become an economic reserve after metallurgy, mine design and capital costs; or permitting/funding needs force severe dilution before feasibility. Reassess with the amended NI 43-101/feasibility work, current cash and full capital register; the C$1.98 Oct13 TSXV last trade is not executable.

Risk / thesis breaker

A large mineral resource does not establish an economic reserve; current cash, legacy dilution, permitting and future US$50m milestone obligations remain unresolved. Thesis fails if: Feasibility or permitting fails to support an economic mine, or financing requirements cause severe common-share dilution.
